People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… hi. I guess what brings me in is, I can’t really keep my words in a straight line anymore. Like, I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like something that happened on an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the color of the walls, or paint colors I saw somewhere, or patient charts, and by the time I look at the person I can tell they’re lost. Like their face changes, and I realize I’ve dragged them through five different things and I don’t even know exactly how I got there.

It’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected at first, like there’s a path, but then when it comes out loud it’s all mixed up. I’ll be trying to answer one question, and then I hear myself saying something else, and then I try to fix it, but that makes it longer and worse. People kind of wait for me to get back to the point, and I can see that happening, and then I get more tangled. That’s the main reason I came in, I think.

straight line anymore, like I start talking about an overnight shift and end up jumping to soccer, paint colors, patient charts, and I can see everyone getting lost.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work I’ve caught myself rocking in the hallway, copying a patient’s tapping, or standing there with my gloves half on like I’m stuck, when I know I’m supposed to be doing rounds.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I feel heavy and empty from the time I wake up, and after shifts I’ll sit in my car crying before I can even drive home.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to sketch murals, cook for my friends, and play soccer on weekends, but now all of that feels pointless and I usually just ignore messages and stay in my apartment.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… hi. I guess what brings me in is, I can’t really keep my words in a straight line anymore. Like, I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like something that happened on an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the color of the walls, or paint colors I saw somewhere, or patient charts, and by the time I look at the person I can tell they’re lost. Like their face changes, and I realize I’ve dragged them through five different things and I don’t even know exactly how I got there.\n\nIt’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected at first, like there’s a path, but then when it comes out loud it’s all mixed up. I’ll be trying to answer one question, and then I hear myself saying something else, and then I try to fix it, but that makes it longer and worse. People kind of wait for me to get back to the point, and I can see that happening, and then I get more tangled. That’s the main reason I came in, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Um… unusually high, like happy-high? I don’t think so. Not really. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m maybe more wound up or I’m talking a lot, but euphoric doesn’t sound right to me. I don’t feel like everything is great or like I’m on top of the world.\n\nIf anything, when people say “high,” I think of someone being excited or confident or like… bright, I guess, and that’s not how it feels from the inside. I’m not sure I’d call it that. Maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean by euphoric, but no, I haven’t noticed feeling especially good in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? Not like a clear voice standing behind me or someone talking in the room when I can look and nobody’s there. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been hearing voices exactly.\n\nLike, if you mean words, actual words, telling me things or talking to me, I don’t think I have that. Yeah… I mean, “harm” sounds maybe too dramatic if you mean like someone physically attacking me, but… yes, I do feel like something is going on at work. I’m pretty sure the supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose, and not just because of staffing like they keep saying. They’ll say, “Oh, we’re short,” or “We had to move people around,” but it keeps happening in a way that feels directed at me.

And the cameras on the unit… I know they’re there for safety, obviously, but I feel like they’re watching me through them specifically, like checking if I’m unsafe or waiting for me to mess up. I’ll be walking down the hall and I can feel myself thinking, okay, they’re looking right now, they’re seeing how I’m moving, how I’m talking to people, whether I look off. People tell me it’s just normal cameras and normal scheduling, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re building some kind of case or testing me without saying it directly.

I don’t think so. I mean, my sleep is not great sometimes, but needing much less sleep and then feeling full of energy… that doesn’t really sound like me.

If I don’t sleep much, I usually feel it. I’m tired, kind of dragging, more on edge maybe, but not energized like I can just keep going and don’t need rest. There might be nights where I’m up later than I meant to be, or I wake up and can’t get settled again, but the next day I’m not like, “Wow, I’m fine.” I’m more trying to get through the day.

So… no, I haven’t noticed a period like that where I barely slept and still felt really powered up or anything.

I’m pretty sure the supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose, and not just because of staffing like they keep saying. They’ll say, “Oh, we’re short,” or “We had to move people around,” but it keeps happening in a way that feels directed at me.\n\nAnd the cameras on the unit… I know they’re there for safety, obviously, but I feel like they’re watching me through them specifically, like checking if I’m unsafe or waiting for me to mess up. Not really apart from that… I mean, that’s the main thing people keep telling me I’m “reading into,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m making it up.

It’s mostly work. The supervisors changing my shifts, and then the cameras on the unit. People say it’s just staffing shortages or that the cameras are just normal safety cameras, and I understand that’s what they’re supposed to be for, but it feels too specific to me. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my sleep is not great sometimes, but needing much less sleep and then feeling full of energy… that doesn’t really sound like me.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually feel it. I’m tired, kind of dragging, more on edge maybe, but not energized like I can just keep going and don’t need rest. There might be nights where I’m up later than I meant to be, or I wake up and can’t get settled again, but the next day I’m not like, “Wow, I’m fine.” I’m more trying to get through the day.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed a period like that where I barely slept and still felt really powered up or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems start after a head injury, seizure, or another medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can connect it to. I mean, I’ve never sat down and thought, “Okay, this all started right after I hit my head,” or after a seizure or some illness like that. I don’t really have a clear thing like that in my mind.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what would count, because people get sick sometimes or have headaches or whatever, but nothing stands out to me like a major medical thing that came first and then everything changed right after. So… as far as I know, no, I haven’t noticed that kind of connection.\n\nDoctor: Apart from your concerns about work, do you have any other strong beliefs that people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Not really apart from that… I mean, that’s the main thing people keep telling me I’m “reading into,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m making it up.\n\nIt’s mostly work. The supervisors changing my shifts, and then the cameras on the unit. People say it’s just staffing shortages or that the cameras are just normal safety cameras, and I understand that’s what they’re supposed to be for, but it feels too specific to me. Like it’s not just random scheduling or normal monitoring. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I have. I’ve kind of pulled away from people a lot, more than I even like admitting, I guess.

I used to be more… I don’t know, involved. Like I used to sketch murals, just for fun, and I’d get really into the ideas for them. I used to cook for friends too — not anything fancy all the time, but I liked having people over, making food, hanging out. And on weekends I’d play soccer, or at least I’d make plans to. It wasn’t like I was some super social person every second, but I had things I cared about, and people I’d actually want to see.

Now it all just feels kind of pointless. Like someone texts me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, but I just… don’t. Then hours go by, or days, and it feels harder to respond because now I have to explain why I didn’t. So I just ignore it. I end up staying in my apartment most of the time.

It’s not always that I hate people or anything. It’s more like being around them feels like too much effort, and the things I used to enjoy don’t really pull me in anymore. I know that sounds bad, but that’s kind of how it’s been.

It’s mainly that situation, but it feels very real to me even when other people say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I’ve kind of pulled away from people a lot, more than I even like admitting, I guess.\n\nI used to be more… I don’t know, involved. Like I used to sketch murals, just for fun, and I’d get really into the ideas for them. I used to cook for friends too — not anything fancy all the time, but I liked having people over, making food, hanging out. And on weekends I’d play soccer, or at least I’d make plans to. It wasn’t like I was some super social person every second, but I had things I cared about, and people I’d actually want to see.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. Like someone texts me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, but I just… don’t. Then hours go by, or days, and it feels harder to respond because now I have to explain why I didn’t. So I just ignore it. I end up staying in my apartment most of the time.\n\nIt’s not always that I hate people or anything. It’s more like being around them feels like too much effort, and the things I used to enjoy don’t really pull me in anymore. I know that sounds bad, but that’s kind of how it’s been.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave other people told you that your face or voice seems less expressive than it used to be?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I’m not really sure. Nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your face looks flat,” or “Your voice sounds different,” not in those exact words.

I mean, sometimes people will say I seem tired, or they’ll ask if I’m okay because I’m being quiet, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as less expressive. I don’t really watch myself talk, you know? And when I’m around people lately, I’m probably not giving them a lot to react to anyway, so it’s hard to tell.

I guess I haven’t noticed it clearly. If it’s happening, I don’t think anyone has explained it to me that way.

I’ll be trying to answer one question, and then I hear myself saying something else, and then I try to fix it, but that makes it longer and worse. manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Apart from your concerns about work, do you have any other strong beliefs that people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Not really apart from that… I mean, that’s the main thing people keep telling me I’m “reading into,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m making it up.\n\nIt’s mostly work. The supervisors changing my shifts, and then the cameras on the unit. People say it’s just staffing shortages or that the cameras are just normal safety cameras, and I understand that’s what they’re supposed to be for, but it feels too specific to me. Like it’s not just random scheduling or normal monitoring. It feels like they’re watching to see if I’m unsafe, or if I’ll do something wrong, or like they’re gathering something on me.\n\nSo… if you mean other beliefs like outside of work, I don’t know. Nothing comes to mind the same way. It’s mainly that situation, but it feels very real to me even when other people say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I’ve kind of pulled away from people a lot, more than I even like admitting, I guess.\n\nI used to be more… I don’t know, involved. Like I used to sketch murals, just for fun, and I’d get really into the ideas for them. I used to cook for friends too — not anything fancy all the time, but I liked having people over, making food, hanging out. And on weekends I’d play soccer, or at least I’d make plans to. It wasn’t like I was some super social person every second, but I had things I cared about, and people I’d actually want to see.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. Like someone texts me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, but I just… don’t. Then hours go by, or days, and it feels harder to respond because now I have to explain why I didn’t. So I just ignore it. I end up staying in my apartment most of the time.\n\nIt’s not always that I hate people or anything. It’s more like being around them feels like too much effort, and the things I used to enjoy don’t really pull me in anymore. I know that sounds bad, but that’s kind of how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your face or voice seems less expressive than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your face looks flat,” or “Your voice sounds different,” not in those exact words.\n\nI mean, sometimes people will say I seem tired, or they’ll ask if I’m okay because I’m being quiet, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as less expressive. I don’t really watch myself talk, you know? And when I’m around people lately, I’m probably not giving them a lot to react to anyway, so it’s hard to tell.\n\nI guess I haven’t noticed it clearly. If it’s happening, I don’t think anyone has explained it to me that way.\n\n[Last Question]\nAbout how long have you been feeling that your supervisors are targeting you at work?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… a little over a year now, I think. Like, not just a few weeks or something.

If I try to place it, it started around maybe a year and a month ago. At first it was smaller things where I’d think, “That’s weird, why did they change my shift like that?” or “Why is that supervisor watching me so closely?” And I tried to tell myself it was probably nothing. But then it kept happening, or it felt like it kept lining up too much, and I couldn’t shake it.

So yeah… about 13 months, I guess. That’s when it really started feeling like they were targeting me and watching me on purpose.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… hi. I guess what brings me in is, I can’t really keep my words in a straight line anymore. Like, I’ll start trying to explain something simple, like something that happened on an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or the color of the walls, or paint colors I saw somewhere, or patient charts, and by the time I look at the person I can tell they’re lost. Like their face changes, and I realize I’ve dragged them through five different things and I don’t even know exactly how I got there.\n\nIt’s frustrating because in my head it feels connected at first, like there’s a path, but then when it comes out loud it’s all mixed up. I’ll be trying to answer one question, and then I hear myself saying something else, and then I try to fix it, but that makes it longer and worse. People kind of wait for me to get back to the point, and I can see that happening, and then I get more tangled. That’s the main reason I came in, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high or euphoric lately?\n\nPatient: Um… unusually high, like happy-high? I don’t think so. Not really. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m maybe more wound up or I’m talking a lot, but euphoric doesn’t sound right to me. I don’t feel like everything is great or like I’m on top of the world.\n\nIf anything, when people say “high,” I think of someone being excited or confident or like… bright, I guess, and that’s not how it feels from the inside. I’m not sure I’d call it that. Maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean by euphoric, but no, I haven’t noticed feeling especially good in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so? Not like a clear voice standing behind me or someone talking in the room when I can look and nobody’s there. I mean, sometimes I get unsure because places can be noisy, especially at work, and you hear carts or people down the hall or someone calling for someone, and then it turns out maybe it wasn’t what I thought. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been hearing voices exactly.\n\nLike, if you mean words, actual words, telling me things or talking to me, I don’t think I have that. Sometimes my own thoughts are loud, if that makes sense, but they feel like mine. So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sure that someone is trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, “harm” sounds maybe too dramatic if you mean like someone physically attacking me, but… yes, I do feel like something is going on at work. I’m pretty sure the supervisors have been changing my shifts on purpose, and not just because of staffing like they keep saying. They’ll say, “Oh, we’re short,” or “We had to move people around,” but it keeps happening in a way that feels directed at me.\n\nAnd the cameras on the unit… I know they’re there for safety, obviously, but I feel like they’re watching me through them specifically, like checking if I’m unsafe or waiting for me to mess up. I’ll be walking down the hall and I can feel myself thinking, okay, they’re looking right now, they’re seeing how I’m moving, how I’m talking to people, whether I look off. People tell me it’s just normal cameras and normal scheduling, but it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels like they’re building some kind of case or testing me without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my sleep is not great sometimes, but needing much less sleep and then feeling full of energy… that doesn’t really sound like me.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually feel it. I’m tired, kind of dragging, more on edge maybe, but not energized like I can just keep going and don’t need rest. There might be nights where I’m up later than I meant to be, or I wake up and can’t get settled again, but the next day I’m not like, “Wow, I’m fine.” I’m more trying to get through the day.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed a period like that where I barely slept and still felt really powered up or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems start after a head injury, seizure, or another medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can connect it to. I mean, I’ve never sat down and thought, “Okay, this all started right after I hit my head,” or after a seizure or some illness like that. I don’t really have a clear thing like that in my mind.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what would count, because people get sick sometimes or have headaches or whatever, but nothing stands out to me like a major medical thing that came first and then everything changed right after. So… as far as I know, no, I haven’t noticed that kind of connection.\n\nDoctor: Apart from your concerns about work, do you have any other strong beliefs that people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Not really apart from that… I mean, that’s the main thing people keep telling me I’m “reading into,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m making it up.\n\nIt’s mostly work. The supervisors changing my shifts, and then the cameras on the unit. People say it’s just staffing shortages or that the cameras are just normal safety cameras, and I understand that’s what they’re supposed to be for, but it feels too specific to me. Like it’s not just random scheduling or normal monitoring. It feels like they’re watching to see if I’m unsafe, or if I’ll do something wrong, or like they’re gathering something on me.\n\nSo… if you mean other beliefs like outside of work, I don’t know. Nothing comes to mind the same way. It’s mainly that situation, but it feels very real to me even when other people say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Like I used to sketch murals, just for fun, and I’d get really into the ideas for them. I used to cook for friends too — not anything fancy all the time, but I liked having people over, making food, hanging out. And on weekends I’d play soccer, or at least I’d make plans to. It wasn’t like I was some super social person every second, but I had things I cared about, and people I’d actually want to see.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. So I just ignore it. I end up staying in my apartment most of the time.\n\nIt’s not always that I hate people or anything. It’s more like being around them feels like too much effort, and the things I used to enjoy don’t really pull me in anymore. I know that sounds bad, but that’s kind of how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your face or voice seems less expressive than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Nobody’s sat me down and said, “Your face looks flat,” or “Your voice sounds different,” not in those exact words.\n\nI mean, sometimes people will say I seem tired, or they’ll ask if I’m okay because I’m being quiet, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as less expressive. Yeah… definitely. It’s gotten to the point where I can’t really pretend it’s just “stress” or being tired anymore.

At work, I’m still showing up, but I’m not keeping up the way I used to. I work as an aide, so there are rounds and charting and all these things you can’t really mess up or forget, and I’ve had coworkers having to double-check me more than they should. Like they’ll look back over my charting or make sure I actually completed rounds the right way, because I’ll get distracted or I’ll second-guess what I wrote, or I’ll just feel like I’m moving through fog. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I know people notice. And then that makes me feel worse, because I’m supposed to be helping, not becoming another problem somebody has to watch.

And at home it’s been worse. Bills pile up because I see them and I just don’t open them. Or I’ll open one and think, “I need to deal with this today,” and then I don’t, and then it becomes this whole thing I avoid. I’ve ignored friends so much that I don’t even know what to say back anymore. My apartment is… I mean, it’s bad. Not like a little messy. Like dishes, laundry, trash, stuff everywhere, and I keep telling myself I’ll do one small part of it, but then I just sit there looking at it and feel overwhelmed.

It got bad enough at work that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide. I didn’t even mean to make it into this huge thing, but I guess when I said it out loud, they took it seriously. So… yeah, it’s definitely interfering with work and just basic life stuff.

Like they’ll look back over my charting or make sure I actually completed rounds the right way, because I’ll get distracted or I’ll second-guess what I wrote, or I’ll just feel like I’m moving through fog. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I know people notice. And then that makes me feel worse, because I’m supposed to be helping, not becoming another problem somebody has to watch.\n\nAnd at home it’s been worse. Bills pile up because I see them and I just don’t open them. Or I’ll open one and think, “I need to deal with this today,” and then I don’t, and then it becomes this whole thing I avoid. I’ve ignored friends so much that I don’t even know what to say back anymore. My apartment is… I mean, it’s bad. Not like a little messy. Like dishes, laundry, trash, stuff everywhere, and I keep telling myself I’ll do one small part of it, but then I just sit there looking at it and feel overwhelmed.\n\nIt got bad enough at work that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide. I didn’t even mean to make it into this huge thing, but I guess when I said it out loud, they took it seriously. I’m tired, kind of dragging, more on edge maybe, but not energized like I can just keep going and don’t need rest. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. Not just “down” like a bad day, but most of the time, especially when it gets really bad.

It’s like the two things feed into each other. When I’m more depressed, that’s when I’m also the most sure my supervisors are watching me, like through the cameras at work. I’ll start thinking every camera is being checked because of me, or that they’re waiting for me to mess something up so they can use it. And then because I’m thinking that, I get more frozen and more ashamed, and then I feel even lower.

And when I’m at my worst, I can’t even explain it right. Like if someone asks me what’s wrong, my words get all tangled. I’ll start a sentence and then lose where I was going, or I’ll try to explain why I think they’re watching me and it comes out messy, like I’m not making sense even though in my head it feels connected. Then I can see people looking confused or concerned, and that makes me feel even more like I’m being watched or judged.

So… yeah. During those times, I’m usually depressed most of the time, and the worse the depression gets, the stronger that feeling about them targeting me gets too.

I mean, I’ve never sat down and thought, “Okay, this all started right after I hit my head,” or after a seizure or some illness like that. I don’t really have a clear thing like that in my mind.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what would count, because people get sick sometimes or have headaches or whatever, but nothing stands out to me like a major medical thing that came first and then everything changed right after. So… as far as I know, no, I haven’t noticed that kind of connection.\n\nDoctor: Apart from your concerns about work, do you have any other strong beliefs that people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Not really apart from that… I mean, that’s the main thing people keep telling me I’m “reading into,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m making it up.\n\nIt’s mostly work. The supervisors changing my shifts, and then the cameras on the unit. People say it’s just staffing shortages or that the cameras are just normal safety cameras, and I understand that’s what they’re supposed to be for, but it feels too specific to me. Like it’s not just random scheduling or normal monitoring. It feels like they’re watching to see if I’m unsafe, or if I’ll do something wrong, or like they’re gathering something on me.\n\nSo… if you mean other beliefs like outside of work, I don’t know. Nothing comes to mind the same way. It’s mainly that situation, but it feels very real to me even when other people say it isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I’ve kind of pulled away from people a lot, more than I even like admitting, I guess.\n\nI used to be more… I don’t know, involved. Like I used to sketch murals, just for fun, and I’d get really into the ideas for them. I used to cook for friends too — not anything fancy all the time, but I liked having people over, making food, hanging out. And on weekends I’d play soccer, or at least I’d make plans to. It wasn’t like I was some super social person every second, but I had things I cared about, and people I’d actually want to see.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. Like someone texts me and I’ll see it, and I know I should answer, but I just… don’t. Then hours go by, or days, and it feels harder to respond because now I have to explain why I didn’t. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… there was a time like that. That’s part of what confused me, honestly, because I kept trying to tell myself it was only when I was really depressed, like when everything felt heavy and hopeless. But there was a stretch — maybe a few weeks — where I wasn’t in that deep, dark place the same way.

I mean, I wasn’t suddenly great or anything, but I could get up a little easier, I wasn’t just sitting there feeling completely crushed all day. I remember thinking, “Okay, maybe I’m coming out of it a little.” But even then, at work, I still felt sure the cameras were being used to watch me. Like I’d walk past one and immediately think, “They’re checking what I’m doing right now,” or if a supervisor came around, I’d feel like it wasn’t normal supervision, it was directed at me.

And I acted different because of it. I was guarded, like careful with every movement, careful what I said, trying not to look suspicious even though I didn’t even know what “suspicious” would be. I’d avoid certain areas if I thought I was being watched too much, or I’d get stiff when a supervisor was nearby.

So… yeah. It wasn’t only during the worst depression. There was at least that few-week period where my mood wasn’t as low, but I still believed they were monitoring me and targeting me.
